cat > /tmp/claude-1000/-home-derek-dev-claude-code-plugins/9d4bafbd-d674-4a91-b9e5-b2657a6ed368/scratchpad/survey.py <<'EOF'
import re,sys,pathlib
home=pathlib.Path.home()
roots="devbox services/<project> dev/claude-code-plugins dev/osu-builder-in-residence projects/<project> projects/<project> projects/daily-briefing projects/<project> projects/airbnb-prep projects/<project> projects/daily-digest projects/log-archaeologist projects/persimmon-care dev/wellstead".split()
H=re.compile(r'^(#{1,6}) +(.*)$')
D=re.compile(r'^(\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2})\b(.*)$')
for r in roots:
    p=home/r/'docs/chronicle/reflective-practice.md'
    lines=p.read_text().splitlines()
    on=False; notes=[]; cur=None; evo=0; fence=0
    for i,l in enumerate(lines,1):
        m=H.match(l)
        if l.startswith('```'): fence+=1
        if m and len(m[1])==2:
            on = m[2].strip().lower().startswith('evolution notes')
            evo+=on; cur=None; continue
        if not on: continue
        if m and len(m[1])>=3:
            d=D.match(m[2])
            if d: cur={'line':i,'date':d[1],'rest':d[2],'body':[]}; notes.append(cur)
            elif len(m[1])==3: 
                print('   NONDATE-H3',r,i,l[:90]); 
            continue
        if cur is not None and l.strip(): cur['body'].append(l)
    print('==',r,'evo sections',evo,'notes',len(notes),'fence lines',fence)
    for n in notes:
        h='deep review' in n['rest'].lower()
        b=' '.join(n['body'])
        hits=[m.start() for m in re.finditer('deep review',b,re.I)]
        if h or hits:
            s=(b[max(0,hits[0]-50):hits[0]+70] if hits else '')
            print(f"  L{n['line']} {n['date']} H={int(h)} nbody={len(hits)} first@{hits[0] if hits else '-'} | {n['rest'][:70]!r} | ...{s!r}")
EOF

gh pr create --base main --title "fix(session-chronicle): count entries since the last deep review" --body-file - <<'EOF'
## Problem

Step 6a counted chronicle files since the newest dated heading under Evolution Notes. Lightweight checks write dated notes too, so each one reset the count and the mandatory 10-entry deep review never fired. `daily-briefing` went from 04-12 to 11-06 without a full deep review, with its count stuck at 0 to 2.

## Fix

- **The anchor is now the newest deep review**, not the newest dated note. Lightweight notes stay dated but no longer affect the count.
- **The count is code.** `scripts/deep-review-status.py` replaces the prose in Step 6a, so it can be tested deterministically. It's Python stdlib with `unittest`, matching session-export.
- **New reviews write `#### YYYY-MM-DD (deep review)`.** Older shapes are recognized strictly, because a false match suppresses a review while a miss only costs one early review.

Decisions and rationale are in `docs/specs/deep-review-anchor.md`.

## Behavior change to review

With no deep review on record, every entry counts. Six of the 14 practice files on the devbox will run one deep review on their next chronicle write, capped at 20 entries: `devbox` (27 entries since 07-12), `claude-code-plugins`, `airbnb-prep`, `daily-digest`, `persimmon-care`, and `wellstead`. `daily-digest` was already due under the old rule. This is why the bump is minor (1.9.0).

## Testing

From `plugins/meta-claude/skills/session-chronicle/scripts`:

```
python3 -m unittest test_deep_review_status.py
```

- The first commit transcribes the old rule literally and fails with 19 failures. The core case reports a count of 0 instead of 10+.
- The second commit passes all 29 tests.
- Eight mutations of a copy all fail the tests, including restoring the old rule (15 failures), `>=` for `>` on the anchor date, an off-by-one threshold, and dropping the marker from the SKILL.md template.
- Run read-only against all 14 real practice files: `daily-briefing` reports 2026-11-06 with a count of 0, and `devbox` reports 2026-07-12 with 27.

Not tested: a live `/session-chronicle` run through the installed plugin. If the script can't run, Step 6a says to report that and do the lightweight check.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

Shown in the next-step box. Your closing message is your answer to the user: write it now, in full, as the last thing you say this turn. The box asks your question, so do not ask it again.

⏺ The review of [PR 77](https://github.com/ddehart/claude-code-plugins/pull/77) found 10 issues, and I'd fix the first two before merging. Most came from running the script against edge-case practice files; none are fixed yet, and all 10 are in the findings panel.

- **Repeated reviews:** a qualifier before the marker, like `#### 2026-12-01 (later) — Deep review`, isn't recognized. The anchor then never moves, so the mandatory review fires on every chronicle write. `devbox` already puts parentheticals right after the date, so this could happen in real use.
- **Suppressed reviews:** some notes that say no review happened still count as one, such as `— Deep review not due` or an opening line of `Escalated this question to the next deep review.` That's the original bug in a narrower form, and it contradicts the strict matching I described in the PR.

A third change would catch most repeat-review cases, whatever the cause: re-run the script after writing a review note and confirm it recognized the note.

The remaining findings are lower risk or cleanup:
- a future-dated note freezes the count at 0
- a variant `Evolution Notes` heading hides every note
- a script failure skips the mandatory review
- unbalanced code fences hide the notes after them
- the repo `CLAUDE.md` still lists only the session-export test
- one small edge case and one small cleanup in the script

> Yes, fix them

A miss can make the review fire again on every write, so the skill re-runs the script after writing a review note and confirms `last_deep_review` is today. Section headings match loosely (`## Evolution notes (newest first)`, `### Evolution Notes`) for the same reason.

fix(session-chronicle): harden deep-review recognition after review

A code review of the count script found reviews it couldn't see, which
would fire again on every write, and notes it wrongly accepted, which
would suppress a review.

- Accept a qualifier before the marker, like `(later) — Deep review`
- Reject notes saying no review ran, like `Deep review not due`
- Ignore future-dated reviews, which would stop the count
- Match section heading variants; close fences by kind and length, and
  let an unclosed fence hide nothing
- Skip directories named like entries; tolerate non-UTF-8 bytes
- Have the skill confirm its review note was recognized, and put a
  script failure first in the report
- List the new tests in CLAUDE.md

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF

⏺ All 10 review findings are fixed and pushed to [PR 77](https://github.com/ddehart/claude-code-plugins/pull/77), and all 38 tests pass. The PR is still unmerged, and the requesting session has the update.

- **Failing first:** 9 new tests covered the review findings, and all of them failed before the fix. I also broke a copy of the code nine ways, one per key rule, and the tests failed each time.
- **Recognition:** `(later) — Deep review` now counts as a review, while `Deep review not due` and `(deep review deferred)` don't.
- **Repeat-review guard:** after writing a review note, the skill re-runs the script and confirms `last_deep_review` is today. If a heading isn't recognized, the session finds out right away.
- **Script failures:** the skill still falls back to the lightweight check, but the error now goes first in the report.
- **Other fixes:**
  - Future-dated notes are ignored.
  - `Evolution Notes` heading variants now match.
  - Unbalanced code fences no longer hide notes.
  - Non-UTF-8 bytes no longer crash the script.
  - The repo `CLAUDE.md` now lists the new tests.

I also ran Step 6a through a real skill load in a scratch project. `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}` expanded and the script returned the right count, which closes most of the earlier gap. A full chronicle write through the installed plugin is still untested, and CI doesn't run these tests.

⏺ Bash(python3 - <<'EOF'
import pathlib
base=pathlib.Path('plugins/meta-claude/skills/session-chronicle')
def sub(path,pairs):
    p=pathlib.Path(path); t=p.read_text()
    for a,b in pairs:
        assert t.count(a)==1,a
        t=t.replace(a,b)
    p.write_text(t)

sub(base/'scripts/deep-review-status.py',[
("Prints JSON: last_deep_review, count, threshold, due, entries (newest first).","Prints JSON: last_deep_review, count, due, entries (newest first)."),
("""# `(deep review)` is the marker SKILL.md writes. The rest are older shapes still
# in practice files: `— Deep review (14 entries...)`, `— Escalated deep review`,
# and plain dated headings over `Deep review ran; ...`, `Deep review at the
# 10-entry threshold`, or `**Escalated from the lightweight check to a deep review.**`
# A parenthetical qualifier may come first, as in `(later) — Deep review`.
REVIEW_HEADING = re.compile(
    r'^(?:[\\s—–:-]*\\([^)]*\\))*'
    r'[\\s(—–:-]*(?:(?:escalated|full) )?deep review\\b'
    r'(?![\\s:;,—–-]*(?:not|deferred|held|skipped|due|overdue|pending|postponed)\\b)',
    re.I,
)
""","""# `(deep review)` is the marker SKILL.md writes. The other shapes come from older
# notes: headings like `— Deep review (14 entries...)`, `— Deep review across 16
# reflections`, and `— Escalated deep review (after a miscount)`, and plain dated
# headings over `Deep review at the 10-entry threshold`, the previous no-op note
# `Deep review ran; ...`, or `**Escalated from the lightweight check to a deep review.**`
# A parenthetical qualifier may come first, as in `(later) (deep review)`.
# What follows `deep review` is an allowlist: `Deep review can wait` must not match.
REVIEW_HEADING = re.compile(
    r'^[\\s—–:-]*(?:\\([^)]*\\)[\\s—–:-]*)*?'
    r'\\(?(?:(?:escalated|full) )?deep review(?:\\)|\\s*$|\\s+\\(|\\s+across\\b)',
    re.I,
)
"""),
("""    if not re.fullmatch(r'\\d{4}-\\d{2}-\\d{2}', text):
        return False
    try:
        date.fromisoformat(text)
    except ValueError:
        return False
    return True
""","""    try:
        return date.fromisoformat(text).isoformat() == text
    except ValueError:
        return False
"""),
("""    (the first line of its body). Dated notes are `###` or `####` headings; older
    practice files use `###`. Fenced code is skipped, so a quoted prompt or
    template can neither add a note nor end the section.
""","""    (the first line of its body). Older practice files date their notes with `###`
    rather than `####`, so any heading inside the section can be a note. Fenced
    code is skipped, so a quoted prompt or template can neither add a note nor end
    the section.
"""),
("            if dated and level >= 3 and is_date(dated[1]):\n","            if dated and is_date(dated[1]):\n"),
("""    chronicle_dir = Path(chronicle_dir)
    practice = chronicle_dir""","""    practice = chronicle_dir"""),
("""    dates = sorted(
        (p.stem for p in chronicle_dir.glob('*.md') if p.is_file() and is_date(p.stem)),
        reverse=True,
    )
    if last_review:
        dates = [entry for entry in dates if entry > last_review]

    return {
        'last_deep_review': last_review,
        'count': len(dates),
        'threshold': THRESHOLD,
        'due': len(dates) >= THRESHOLD,
        'entries': [f'{entry}.md' for entry in dates],

        (p.name for p in chronicle_dir.glob('*.md')

    )

    return {
        'last_deep_review': last_review,
        'count': len(entries),
        'due': len(entries) >= THRESHOLD,
        'entries': entries,
    }
"""),
])

⏺ # Security Review: `fix/chronicle-deep-review-anchor`

No vulnerabilities found. The review read the code and did not run the script, so no false-positive filtering step was needed.

**Checked in `plugins/meta-claude/skills/session-chronicle/scripts/deep-review-status.py`:**

- **Code execution and injection:** it imports only `json`, `re`, `sys`, `datetime` and `pathlib`. It has no subprocess, `eval`, `pickle` or YAML, and makes no writes or network calls.
- **Shell path:** the `SKILL.md` command takes a fixed `docs/chronicle` argument and the trusted `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}`. No file content reaches a shell.
- **Path traversal:** the only path input is the trusted CLI argument, the practice filename is fixed, and `glob('*.md')` doesn't recurse.
- **Data exposure:** the output holds only `last_deep_review`, `count`, `due` and `entries`. Note text is never printed, and anything that isn't a valid date is dropped, so crafted filenames or notes can't put free text in the output.
- **Module shadowing:** `python3 <path>` puts the script's directory on `sys.path`, not the working directory, so a planted `json.py` in a project root isn't imported.
- **Other changes:** the manifest diffs are version bumps only, and no hooks were added.

A forged note can suppress a deep review, but that only affects how often reviews run and has no security impact.

- **Five checker changes after seeing results:** four verdicts flipped to pass. The two judgment calls are the ordering check, which accepts a script run anywhere after the entry is written, and the void rule, which now needs a denied script or `agy` execution. The report gives the reason for each.

No skill defects turned up, and nothing is committed. The requesting session has the full report and can now fix its stale sentence. The test runs left 17 small files under `~/.claude/projects`, which are safe to delete.

# session-chronicle skill

`meta-claude:session-chronicle`. Writes the two-layer session journal in `docs/chronicle/` (a neutral

Step 6 picks a review tier. Since 1.9.0 the count that decides it is a script,
`scripts/deep-review-status.py`, which counts daily files since the newest **deep review** and prints JSON.
A deep review writes `#### YYYY-MM-DD (deep review)` and then re-runs the script to confirm it was
recognized. Lightweight notes no longer move the count. See

# Decision
write("decisions/session-chronicle counts entries since the newest deep review, in tested code.md", f"""
---
genitor: "[[decisions]]"
tags: [session-chronicle, testing, prose-as-instructions]
---

# session-chronicle counts entries since the newest deep review, in tested code

The mandatory 10-entry deep review never fired, because the count anchored on the newest *dated*
Evolution Note, and lightweight checks write dated notes too. The anchor is now the newest note that
records a deep review, and new reviews write a canonical `(deep review)` marker.

**The count is code, not prose.** Step 6a was an instruction a model followed, so no test could be
deterministic, and models had already miscounted it by hand. An eval was rejected, because it would test
one model's reading of the prose rather than the rule. The script has unit tests, including ones that hold
`SKILL.md`'s note template to what the script recognizes.

**With no review on record, every entry counts.** Falling back to the newest dated note would have kept
the bug in exactly the projects it hurt most. The cost is one review per project, which is why the bump
was minor.

**Recognition of older shapes is an allowlist.** What may follow `deep review` in a heading is named, not
the words that negate it, since that list could never be complete. Matching only the new marker was
proposed and rejected: it would report a project's real older review as missing.

*Decided {D}. Spec: `docs/specs/deep-review-anchor.md`.*

## related
- [[ambiguous prose in an LLM-executed skill is a correctness bug]]
- [[a list of negating phrasings cannot be complete, so allow what may follow]]
- [[session-chronicle skill]]
""")

# New patterns
write("patterns/a list of negating phrasings cannot be complete, so allow what may follow.md", f"""
---
genitor: "[[patterns]]"
tags: [recognition, natural-language, strictness]
---

# a list of negating phrasings cannot be complete, so allow what may follow

Recognizing a phrase in free text while rejecting the cases that deny it invites a list of negating words:
"not due", "deferred", "held". Writers have more ways to say no than any list holds, and every missed
phrasing is accepted as the thing it denies. Naming what may follow the phrase inverts the failure: an
unlisted phrasing is rejected instead, which is visible and recoverable when something downstream checks
for it.

*One witness ({D}). Open.*

## evidence
- [[a negation word list let a new phrasing through, and an allowlist replaced it]]

## related
- [[a fail-safe argument holds only if a miss happens once]]
""")
write("patterns/a fail-safe argument holds only if a miss happens once.md", f"""
---
genitor: "[[patterns]]"
tags: [failure-direction, rationale, strictness]
---

# a fail-safe argument holds only if a miss happens once

"Be strict: a false match is the bad direction, and a miss only costs one extra run" is sound only if the
miss corrects itself. When the missed record is the very thing the next run looks for, the miss recurs on
every run. The safe direction then becomes a loop that never ends and never errors. Before choosing a
failure direction, trace the miss through the next cycle and check that something closes it.

*One witness ({D}). Open.*

## evidence
- [[the claim that a missed review note costs one early review was wrong]]

## related
- [[a list of negating phrasings cannot be complete, so allow what may follow]]
- [[justifying prose drifts from the thing it justifies]]
""")
write("patterns/a checker changed after seeing results needs each change stated with its reason.md", f"""
---
genitor: "[[patterns]]"
tags: [testing, evaluation, disclosure]
---

# a checker changed after seeing results needs each change stated with its reason

When a test harness is revised after it has graded runs, every revision is a chance to move the line to
where the results already are. Some revisions are genuine bug fixes in the checker, and they look exactly
like the other kind. The reader can only tell them apart from a per-change account: what changed, which
verdicts flipped, and why the old check was wrong. Re-running the checker's own self-test after the last
edit shows it still fails on tampered input. A summary that says "four now pass" hides the one change that
didn't deserve to land.

*One witness ({D}). Open.*

## evidence
- [[five checker changes after seeing results flipped four verdicts]]

## related
- [[a check inherits the frame of whoever briefed it]]
""")

# Question
write("questions/how should a peer-briefed session record where its instructions came from.md", f"""
---
genitor: "[[questions]]"
tags: [process, provenance, coordination]
---
## question
When another session briefs the work and relays the owner's approvals, those briefs live in the peer's
transcript. This session's transcript shows only its outgoing reports and its replies. Processing it
alone yields a record of work with no visible request, and approvals whose scope can't be checked. Should
the source note link the peer's session, should the peer's brief be preserved with it, or is the outgoing
side enough?

## evidence
- [[this session's transcript holds only the outgoing side of a peer's coordination]]
""")

def add_entries(path, entries, section_start=None, section_end=None):
    p=Path(path); lines=p.read_text().split('\n')
    lo = 0 if section_start is None else lines.index(section_start)+1
    hi = len(lines) if section_end is None else next(i for i in range(lo,len(lines)) if lines[i]==section_end)
    for e in entries:
        key=e.split(']]')[0].lower()
        idx=[i for i in range(lo,hi) if lines[i].startswith('- [[')]
        pos=next((i for i in idx if lines[i].split(']]')[0].lower()>key), (idx[-1]+1) if idx else hi)
        lines.insert(pos,e); hi+=1
    p.write_text('\n'.join(lines))

⏺ Bash(P=/tmp/claude-1000/-home-derek-dev-claude-code-plugins/9d4bafbd-d674-4a91-b9e5-b2657a6ed368/scratchpad/promote; mkdir -p $P && cd $P && cat > "a rule an llm executes from prose cannot be tested until it is code.md" <<'EOF'
---
genitor: "[[claims]]"
tags: [prose-as-code, testing, verification]
date: 2026-11-08
domain: claude-code-plugins
supports: ["[[how should instructions be written when an llm will execute them]]"]
---
An instruction told a model how to count records since an anchor, and the anchor was wrong. The bug had
sat in prose for months: nothing could show it failing, because each run was one model's reading of the
sentence. Moving the count into a small script made it testable. Its first commit transcribed the old rule
literally, and the tests failed on it before the fix landed. An evaluation of the prose was considered and
rejected, because it would have tested a reading of the rule rather than the rule.

What stayed prose (when to run the count, what to write afterward) still needed testing. That took sampled
end-to-end runs with a control: the previous version, run on the same input, had to fail the same checks.
Without a control that fails, a passing sample says nothing about whether the checks can catch anything.

When a rule an LLM follows decides something, move the deciding part into code a test can hold, and test the
remaining prose with sampled runs and a control that must fail. A pass from sampled runs shows the
instructions can work, not that they always will.
EOF
cat > "a list of the ways to deny a phrase accepts every denial it does not list.md" <<'EOF'
---
genitor: "[[claims]]"
tags: [recognition, natural-language, silent-failure]
date: 2026-11-08
domain: claude-code-plugins
supports: ["[[what makes a failure invisible to the surfaces built to catch it]]"]
---
A recognizer had to accept free-text notes recording that an event happened and reject notes saying it
hadn't. The first fix listed negating words to exclude: "not due," "deferred," "held." The next review found
"can wait" still matched. An unlisted way of saying no was accepted as the thing it denied, with no error,
and that suppressed exactly what the recognizer existed to trigger.

Writers have more ways to deny a phrase than any list holds, so a denylist fails silently and forever in
the accepting direction. Naming what may follow the phrase inverts the failure: an unlisted phrasing is
rejected, which shows up wherever something downstream checks that the expected record exists.

When recognizing a phrase in free text, allowlist what may follow it rather than denylisting what negates
it. Then make sure something notices the rejections.
EOF
cat > "a fail-safe direction is safe only if the miss it accepts does not recur.md" <<'EOF'
---
genitor: "[[claims]]"
tags: [failure-direction, gates, rationale]
date: 2026-11-08
domain: claude-code-plugins
supports: ["[[which direction of an adjustment should carry the burden of proof]]"]
---
Strict matching was chosen with an argument about failure direction: a false match suppresses a needed
action, while a miss only costs one extra run, after which the record is rewritten in the form the matcher
recognizes. The argument went into a docstring, a specification, and a change description. Then a review
traced a miss through a second cycle. When the rewritten record was itself not recognized, the "one extra
run" happened on every cycle, with no error, indefinitely.

The cheap direction is cheap only if something closes the miss it accepts. Where the corrective step
depends on the same recognition that missed, the miss recurs, and the safe direction becomes a loop that
never ends and never reports.

Before choosing which direction a check should fail in, trace one miss through the next cycle and name what
closes it. A step that re-checks the corrective write (here, re-running the matcher after writing the
record) turns a recurring miss back into a one-time one.
EOF

from pathlib import Path
claims = {
 "a fail-safe direction is safe only if the miss it accepts does not recur": ("which direction of an adjustment should carry the burden of proof", "the cheap direction is cheap only if something closes the miss it accepts; trace one miss through the next cycle", "**second instance.** A matcher chose the cheap failure direction on the argument that a miss costs one extra run. def insert_sorted(path, entry, start_marker=None):
    p=Path(path); lines=p.read_text().split('\n')
    key=entry.split(']]')[0].lower()
    idx=[i for i,l in enumerate(lines) if l.startswith('- [[')]
    if start_marker:
        s=lines.index(start_marker); idx=[i for i in idx if i>s]
        e=next((i for i in range(s+1,len(lines)) if lines[i].startswith('## ')), len(lines))
        idx=[i for i in idx if i<e]
    pos=next((i for i in idx if lines[i].split(']]')[0].lower()>key), idx[-1]+1)
    lines.insert(pos, entry); p.write_text('\n'.join(lines))
for title,(q,gloss,evid) in claims.items():
    insert_sorted('maps/claims.md', f'- [[{title}]] — {gloss}')
    qp=f'questions/{q}.md'
    p=Path(qp); lines=p.read_text().split('\n')
    i=lines.index('## evidence'); j=i+1
    while j<len(lines) and (lines[j].startswith('- ') or lines[j].strip()=='' and j==i+1): j+=1
    while lines[j-1].strip()=='' and j-1>i: j-=1
    lines.insert(j, f'- [[{title}]] (claude-code-plugins) — {evid}')
    p.write_text('\n'.join(lines))
cl=Path('changelog.md'); t=cl.read_text().rstrip('\n')

import re,pathlib
n=0;elig=[]
for p in sorted(pathlib.Path('questions').glob('*.md')):
    t=p.read_text()
    if '## question' not in t: continue
    n+=1
    ev=t.split('## evidence',1)[1] if '## evidence' in t else ''
    doms={m for m in re.findall(r'\]\] \(([^)]+)\)',ev)}
    if len(doms)>=2: elig.append((p.stem,len(doms)))
print('open questions',n,'with 2+ domains',len(elig))
for s,d in elig[:6]: print(' ',d,s[:90])
EOF)

⏺ Mcp  Ccd Turn  Wrap Up(recap: Nothing reliably prompts a question's graduation. The only trigger is a one-line offer from the commons' own skill, and 72 of the commons' 141 open questions already have evidence from two or more domains.

Shown in the next-step box. Your closing message is your answer to the user: write it now, in full, as the last thing you say this turn. The box asks your question, so do not ask it again.

⏺ Nothing reliably prompts it. I checked the commons' skill, its spec and this machine's schedulers, and here's all there is:

- **A one-line offer from the commons' own skill.** Its knowledge-graph skill can suggest *"Worth promoting [question] to a principle now that [domain] backs it too?"* That only happens in a session working inside the commons, it's offered once, and it's dropped if declined. A `/process` run from another repo, like this one, never loads that skill.
- **A mention in the promotion proposal.** The promote skill says to call out a second domain's first evidence explicitly. That's the note I gave you, and it lives only in this conversation and the commit message.
- **Nothing scheduled.** The original commons spec planned a check that would flag questions with evidence from two domains. That spec was superseded when the validator was dropped, and no cron job or timer covers it.

So in practice a question graduates when someone happens to do it by hand. One past commit did, alongside a promotion: "graduate the metering question".

The backlog shows the gap. 72 of the commons' 141 open questions already have evidence from two or more domains, and only 4 principles exist. One question carries a review note ("eligible; reviewed 2026-07-21 and held"), which is the only sign a review ever ran.

A fix would add a graduation review the commons' own tooling surfaces. One option is a queue of eligible questions, the way `/process` lists unprocessed sessions. That's design work, so it would start with a spec.

> Where are you proposing this graduation skill to live?

⏺ In the `knowledge-commons` plugin, which lives in this repo under `plugins/knowledge-commons`, not in the commons' own generated skill. It would be two pieces.

1. **A step at the end of `promote`.** `promote` is the only thing that adds evidence to the commons, so it's the moment a question becomes eligible. It already detects that case and mentions it in the proposal, as it did for the burden-of-proof question. The new step would turn that mention into a proposal in the same run: here's the question, the evidence from both domains, and the draft "so what" for graduating it in place. You'd approve or skip it. This catches every new case when it happens.

2. **A small `graduate` skill for the backlog.** It would list a graph's open questions that have evidence from two or more domains, then walk them one at a time with the same proposal and an approve-or-hold choice. Each review would be recorded in the map, the way "eligible; reviewed 2026-07-21 and held" is now, so a held question doesn't come back next time. That clears the 72 now waiting and anything a skipped proposal leaves behind.

**Why not the commons' own knowledge-graph skill:** promotions run from the source graph's session, which never loads that skill. That's why the one trigger that exists today never fires. Putting the logic there would keep it in the place nothing visits.

**Why the plugin rather than a one-off for the commons:** every graph the plugin generates has questions that graduate. This repo's graph has five, and they graduate into patterns or decisions. A plugin skill reaches all of them without regenerating anyone's skills.

This is distinct from `promote`'s existing commons-to-rules branch, which turns an already-held principle into a rule. That would stay separate.

Shown in the next-step box. Your closing message is your answer to the user: write it now, in full, as the last thing you say this turn. The box asks your question, so do not ask it again.

⏺ A principle becomes steering only when you turn it into a rule in `~/.claude/rules/`, and nothing does that on its own.

**How it works now**
1. You run `/promote` on a principle from the commons. The commons' config names `~/.claude/rules/` as where its promotions go, so this takes promote's commons-to-rules step.
2. That step reads every existing rule first. If one already covers the principle, it reports "already steering" and stops.
3. It asks whether the principle should change how a session *behaves*, not just what it *knows*. True and interesting isn't enough.
4. It drafts a rule file stating the principle, why it holds (citing its evidence domains in prose), and how to apply it. You approve or skip, and only then does it write.

**What prompts it:** only the graduation step. When a graduation produces a principle no rule covers, it marks the note "Not yet steering" and names promote's rules step as the next move, but never runs it. The principles map has 8 notes marked that way: the 7 from today's walk, plus one from October.

**A gap to fix before using it here.** Your `~/.claude/rules/` is chezmoi-managed: 35 rules, with the source in `~/.local/share/chezmoi/dot_claude/rules`, and the folder isn't a git repo. Promote's rules step doesn't know that. It would write the rendered file directly, find no repo to commit to, and the next `chezmoi apply` would overwrite it. A session following your global instructions might catch this and edit the chezmoi source instead, but nothing in promote makes it.

The fix is small: before writing, promote checks `chezmoi source-path` for the target and, if chezmoi manages it, edits the source, applies it and commits the chezmoi repo. Rules also follow your convention of a short directive, with the full history in `~/.claude/rules-history/`, so the drafted rule should be shaped that way too.

> Yes, fix it here

from pathlib import Path
p=Path('plugins/knowledge-commons/skills/promote/SKILL.md'); t=p.read_text()
a="""  prose (rules directories don't resolve wikilinks, so no `[[links]]` here) — and states how to apply it.

"""
b="""  prose (rules directories don't resolve wikilinks, so no `[[links]]` here) — and states how to apply it.
  Same shape a human-authored rule would take. Read two or three neighboring rules for that shape and
  length. If they keep only a short directive and point to fuller history elsewhere, the new rule does the
  same: the short form here, and the full text where they point.
- **Find where the rule is actually written.** A rules directory is often generated by a dotfiles manager
  rather than edited in place, and a file written into the generated copy is overwritten by the next
  apply. Before proposing, run `chezmoi source-path <rules-dir>`. If it prints a path, chezmoi manages the
  directory, and that path is where the rule file goes. Name it the way its neighbors are named there,
  since chezmoi encodes attributes like templating in the filename. If the command fails because the
  directory isn't managed, or chezmoi isn't installed, the directory itself is the source. Say which one
  you found in the proposal.
"""
assert t.count(a)==1; t=t.replace(a,b)
a="""**A rejected push means the target moved while you were writing.**"""
b="""**For a rules directory, commit where the source lives.** A rules directory is rarely a git repository
itself. When §4 found a chezmoi source, write the rule there, run `chezmoi apply <rendered-path>`, and
confirm the rendered file matches what you wrote. Then commit and push the chezmoi source repository
(`chezmoi git -- add <path>`, then `commit` and `push`), naming the path. Writing, applying, and
committing are one unit: a source edit left uncommitted can be overwritten by the next apply run from
another machine. When the directory is its own source and isn't in a repository, say plainly that the rule
exists only on this machine.

**A rejected push means the target moved while you were writing.**"""
assert t.count(a)==1; t=t.replace(a,b)
p.write_text(t)
for f in ('plugins/knowledge-commons/.claude-plugin/plugin.json','.claude-plugin/marketplace.json'):

    if f.endswith('plugin.json'):
        assert s.count('"version": "0.8.1"')==1; s=s.replace('"version": "0.8.1"','"version": "0.8.2"')
    else:
        i=s.index('"name": "knowledge-commons"'); j=s.index('"version": "0.8.1"',i); s=s[:j]+'"version": "0.8.2"'+s[j+len('"version": "0.8.1"'):]
    q.write_text(s)

⏺ Bash(git add .claude-plugin/marketplace.json plugins/knowledge-commons/.claude-plugin/plugin.json plugins/knowledge-commons/skills/promote/SKILL.md && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'
fix(knowledge-commons): write promoted rules through chezmoi when it manages them

Promote's instruction-tier branch wrote the rule file straight into the
rules directory and committed only if that directory was a git repo. A
chezmoi-managed rules directory is neither: the file lands in the
generated copy, nothing commits it, and the next `chezmoi apply`
overwrites it.

Section 4 now runs `chezmoi source-path` on the rules directory before
proposing, and writes the rule into the source when chezmoi manages it.
It also matches the neighbors' shape, including a short directive with
history kept elsewhere. Section 6 applies the rule, checks the rendered
copy, and commits the chezmoi source as one unit.

git push -q -u origin fix/promote-rules-through-chezmoi 2>&1 | tail -1; gh pr create --base main --title "fix(knowledge-commons): write promoted rules through chezmoi when it manages them" --body-file - <<'EOF'
## Problem

When `promote` turns a commons principle into a rule (§4, the instruction-tier branch), it writes the rule file directly into the rules directory. Then §6 commits only if the target is a git repository.

On a machine where chezmoi manages `~/.claude/rules/`, that directory is a generated copy and not a repository. The new rule would be written there, never committed, and overwritten by the next `chezmoi apply`. Graduation now marks new principles "not yet steering" and points at this step, so it's about to get used.

## Fix

- **§4:** before proposing, run `chezmoi source-path <rules-dir>`. If it prints a path, the rule goes into that source directory, named the way its neighbors are named there. If the command fails, the directory is its own source. The proposal says which one was found. Also, read neighboring rules for shape and length, and if they keep a short directive with history elsewhere, follow that.
- **§6:** for a rules directory, write the source file, run `chezmoi apply <rendered-path>`, confirm the rendered copy matches, then commit and push the chezmoi source repo, as one unit. When the directory is its own source and isn't in a repository, say plainly that the rule exists only on this machine.

Checked on the devbox:
- `chezmoi source-path ~/.claude/rules` prints the source directory and exits 0.
- An unmanaged path exits 1 with "not managed".
- `chezmoi git -- status` works.

The skill hasn't been run end to end, since no promotion to rules has run yet.

Patch bump to knowledge-commons 0.8.2: a correction to existing behavior, nothing new to do. `promote` is a plugin skill, not a template, so no `/graph-patch` delta is needed.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF)
